## Question and authorized scope

Can an agent starting from AGENTS.md discover differences between the actual implementation and the accepted product, select a bounded change, verify it and update the remaining gaps without this chat or GitHub Issues?

The owner required Cycle 0028 to finish before this cycle. PR #45 and closure PR #47 merged, Issue #44 closed, and main CI runs `36226063871`/`36226564710` passed. The broad goal was paused. The owner then authorized documentation first, followed by GStack design review, one matching visual specification and a local instruction harness. The latest request adds four-agent review, explicit gap detection and wording/file reduction.

The owner authorizes extension implementation, starting with removal of all bundled demonstration resources. Preserve real-project runtime, source, recording/replay and saved-learning behavior. Final acceptance remains a separate gate.

## Entry and source-gap baseline

Read the full goal, three feature contracts, implementation guide, current state/cycle, visual authority and applicable development/design/skill contracts. Actual source was inspected read-only at `273660c`; that specification baseline preceded the authorized product-source removal below. [PROJECT_STATE.md's 13-row ledger](https://github.com/laststance/codebook/blob/ef38585a32a1632c5af5d89ae5c4e08cf866fefc/PROJECT_STATE.md#current-product-gaps) records inspected files/symbols, concrete mismatches, UNKNOWNs, separate missing proof and next actions for every AC row. All version 9 integrated host/browser checks remain NOT RUN.

Viewed the actual local v9 PNG through the image-viewing tool. SHA-256: `f189ee82541a72ed981e3f7a0e21bd35a19b6601df1c7208671117a335caf2aa`; 1024 × 1536, 1,878,402 bytes. Observations: native debugger left/app above source center/chat right with bottom save; replay retains that composition with summary replacing chat, recorded debug state and a gap marker; independent Wiki has chapters, sources, Q&A and update/output controls; the bottom strip shows shortcut tooltip and searchable help. PostgreSQL returns to createInvoice, which owns revalidation/redirect. No app/database/debugger was executed for the illustration.

Concrete source gaps discovered, distinct from missing tests:

- Bundled skill starts every request with codebook_status and retains old-mode guidance; independent Wiki cannot follow that prerequisite.
- Replay renders a custom Captured values table. A historical native debugger path is not established by that implementation.
- Frame-gap projection retains the latest source/debug observation; existing gap handling does not establish 4A's independent missing-history clearing.
- Wiki Refresh reopens a saved editor article; it is not browser-triggered agent regeneration.
- At that baseline, the build packaged a demonstration; removal below supersedes this finding. Old presentation selectors remain. The specified save/help controls are not established in the inspected paths.

These observations seed future implementation choices; this documentation review does not implement them or grant runtime PASS.

## Review loop and corrections

Skills: owner-invoked GStack `/review`, `writing-for-agents`; OCR and CodeRabbit per D028. Four in-host reviewers cover adversarial instruction flow, normative completeness, current-source gaps and fresh-agent navigation/wording. They share the harness, so agreement is not cross-provider proof.

First pass at frozen `273660c` found:

1. Selected-row comparison could bypass a whole-product source-gap baseline.
2. Mandatory replay/Wiki contracts still said visual approval was pending; behavioral design still called completed design review the next step and Activity Bar proposed.
3. Mandatory reads included superseded questions/chronology; state contained about 138,230 characters and visual identity was split across files.

Corrections: require all-row audits on new goals/cycles/unassessed source changes and affected-row audits thereafter; seed the source-grounded ledger; require selection/closure to identify before/after gaps; point approval/current work to their authorities; move chronology to immutable Git history; consolidate visual authority into DESIGN; remove nine tracked obsolete/redundant design files. Keep the approved PNG/current prompt, complete feature contracts and required runtime/UI-state branch links.

Round 2: all four reviewers confirmed the source-gap procedure and requirement preservation. Two minor defects remained: concatenated metadata headers and PRODUCT_GOAL's unconditional behavioral-design read contradicting AGENTS's conditional branch. Both are corrected. A fresh-agent walkthrough derived a concrete AC-SKILL/AC-WIKI increment and disconnected-MCP verification while keeping AC-LIVE regression and final acceptance separate. This is a procedure simulation, not evidence that a less capable model implemented the product.

The final zero-edit review receipt is `artifacts/cycle-0029/harness-review-final.json`; it binds the reviewed diff and each reviewer's result. Confirm its actual verdict/identity before delivery; if missing or stale, rerun the applicable review. Future product gaps remain in the ledger.

Scope check: documentation/design/harness and review-policy alignment only; no product-source or runtime change. Initial full reads fell from 12 to 9 Markdown files (including AGENTS); runtime/UI/skill/delivery contracts load for their explicit work branches. Nine obsolete/redundant design files were removed, with their exact bytes recoverable at `273660c`. Current requirements, approved PNG and v9 prompt remain local.

Cart application source/build copies, demo launchers, live profile discovery, action routes and packaged assets disappear. Shared debugger/CDP/evidence/capture utilities and real-project browser execution remain usable. Historical learning data is preserved as data, not a runnable demo.

Procedure: inventory dependencies and archive identities before deletion; remove active targets and migrate retained shared utilities; update schemas/discovery/UI/help/skill/package inputs; compile and run relevant generic/configured/recording/migration tests; produce and inspect a VSIX for bundled target assets and demo launchers. Falsifiers: any bundled application source or live demo profile remains; a real-project path starts a cart target; saved learning is deleted or silently rewritten; shared debugging/recording regressions; packaging builds a removed fixture. Keep failed observations and fix them before claiming this increment complete.

### Bounded evidence reads — selected repair

AC-LIVE/SAVE/LEARNING/SKILL: the installed Auto trial could not open ~300KB snapshot/context responses via Copilot's temporary-file bridge. Existing full resources remain unchanged for compatibility. Full history remains at its existing URI.

Both new resources return UTF-8 JSON text chunks bounded to 4096 bytes, with digest, byte offset, total length and a returned nextUri. Concatenate chunks before parsing or claiming full inspection. A request with an old digest, invalid cursor, split code point or noncanonical URI fails explicitly; no cache can outlive the original authorization/content check. Context pages revalidate the original context hash on every read. No execution, source changes or learning decisions result from reading.

Review findings: preserve full-resource compatibility (P1, confidence 10); do not merely split the entire 300KB graph into dozens of calls when only facts/learning are needed (P1, confidence 10); bind every page to unchanged content/context and keep multibyte text intact (P1, confidence 9). Reuse canonical resource guards, source/hash helpers and RecordingStore validation. This records the authorized repair and bounded technical analysis; full GStack DX/engineering review workflows are not marked complete. Broad persona/competitive-target changes are outside this repair.

### Storage capacity recovery — frozen increment

AC-SAVE/LEARNING/REPLAY: the 16-entry admission limit prevented the real learner from saving new sessions; no deletion/recovery action was available. Add one ordinary Codebook setting, `savedStudyLimit`, default 64 and integer range 1–256, shared by new study and recording admission. Read it at each locked admission so changing it needs no restart. Keep a separate fixed 256-entry catalog safety bound. Lowering the admission limit or invalid configuration must not hide prior history or prevent updates to already admitted studies. Never evict recordings, rewrite progress, enlarge per-recording byte/frame budgets or silently clamp invalid values. Full-library errors name the setting and explain that existing records were kept.

Existing internal read-only stores need no configuration. Freeze tests: seventeenth real storage entry succeeds by default; small limits reject new IDs under the existing cross-window lock; increasing permits retry without recreating stores; lowering preserves reads and existing checkpoint writes; invalid values reject only new admission; oversized configuration and per-recording overrides remain rejected. Package and normal installed dashboard verification follow focused checks. This is bounded storage recovery, not deletion/export, pagination or final acceptance. Earlier failed native records remain intact. Formal whole-cycle engineering/DX review remains pending; this section is the selected implementation analysis, not a completed GStack review.

### Required-frame renderer boundary — selected diagnostic

Primary AC-LIVE, dependent AC-SAVE/AC-DESKTOP. Two normal Cursor development runs on the designated dashboard produced valid, hash-matching 720×640 PNGs for the pre-click phase, yet `BrowserViewport` sent the required frame and never received a paint ACK within the current three-second bound. Starting action: one normal installed Cursor `/codebook` trial with the same fictional login and no source answers supplied. Expected: if a required frame fails, its private event trace identifies the last stage (`received`, `decoded`, `paint scheduled` or decode failure) and visibility/size without logging pixels or input. An unvalidated stage granting ACK or logging frame data falsifies this diagnostic. Do not claim animation proof from telemetry; native video/frames remain required for eventual visual acceptance.

The renderer now emits `frame-stage` for required frames only: received, decoded/decode-failed, queued for paint and first animation frame. Each receipt carries the frame ID, page visibility and a Boolean nonzero-area check; it carries no image bytes, DOM text or input values. `BrowserViewport` accepts only these bounded same-epoch stage messages as private events; the original `painted` message remains the sole acknowledgement. A focused negative-control test verifies a valid diagnostic does not complete capture, a foreign epoch is ignored and extra data is not persisted. Its first run hit only a cross-realm test comparison issue; the corrected focused test and TypeScript build pass. The full extension suite is running. No native stage observation has been made on this source cut yet.

### Flat two-row replay layout — selected correction

Further native inspection changed that hypothesis. Cursor's View: Zoom Out was used twice from the existing enlarged window, without changing its saved settings file. The old unequal split stayed retained. With the bottom panel hidden, Codebook's Restore still could not certify it. Cursor's supported **View: Two Rows Editor Layout** immediately showed the same 80.91 s application frame above captured source, with native debugger left and Learning summary right; no target input or seek occurred. Codebook's own Restore afterward still reported an unconfirmed split. The selected recording and dashboard Git state were preserved. This proves the host can display the required topology in this window; it does not by itself certify Codebook's layout receipt or fresh-profile defaults.

Root-cause hypothesis grounded in source and prior native learning: `LIVE_EDITOR_LAYOUT` is a flat two-row descriptor, but `toPixelEditorLayout` rejects every flat measured grid because it requires a nested cross-axis size. `StudyWorkspace.arrange` therefore always sends fractional 0.5/0.5 row sizes for live/replay. A prior native investigation found that VS Code/Cursor may leave a group at its minimum rather than expand a fractional request. Manual Two Rows uses the host's own equal-row command and succeeds here. This still needs a red/green regression and installed replay proof before calling the hypothesis confirmed for this run.

Primary AC-REPLAY/AC-DESKTOP; dependent AC-ENTRY/AC-LEARNING. Frozen correction: allow `toPixelEditorLayout` to scale a **flat requested layout** from its measured primary axis without requiring a cross axis. Keep the existing rejection for a nested request measured from a flat grid, so three-pane ownership cannot be guessed. `StudyWorkspace.arrange` then supplies absolute row sizes and retains its strict final receipt/foreign-group checks. A regression must fail on the old utility and pass after the change for a clamped 70/1200 two-row input. Run the relevant layout/workspace tests, build and package, then install in Cursor and reopen the same saved dashboard replay with the short panel and summary, verifying application/source/native debugger/summary and 44.33 s ↔ 80.91 s without another application input. A collapsed row, false receipt, moved unrelated pane, stale historical state or changed target files falsifies the correction. The owner's zoom setting must be restored after native testing; VS Code and fixed-nine acceptance remain separate.

Node/Vitest capability source audit: `startIntentSchema` in `extension/src/schemas.ts` permits only `repository-browser`; `StudyService.start` in `extension/src/service.ts` rejects other profiles before reserving a run. `StudyService.prepare` assumes a repository-book-backed configured definition. `createEnvironment` in `extension/src/environment.ts` always creates `ConfiguredBrowserRun`, and its readiness requires Chrome plus external Node and a Next development workspace. `repositoryConfigSchemas.ts` hard-codes `adapter: 'next-dev-webpack'`, and `ConfiguredBrowserRun.prepareOwned` launches a Next development server, reads `.next/dev/lock` and generated bundles, starts Chrome, and waits for DOM before arming stops. Extending only the profile enum would therefore create a false positive; the Node test route needs a separate runtime adapter and capture presentation. Official [Vitest debugging guidance](https://vitest.dev/guide/learn/debugging-tests) supports `--inspect-brk --no-file-parallelism` for a debuggable test worker; [VS Code Node debugging documentation](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/nodejs/nodejs-debugging) supports explicit launch configurations and a JavaScript Debug Terminal. Context7 was queried as requested but returned its monthly quota response, so these official primary references were used for this design audit.

Frozen implementation direction for the next source increment, primary G03/G04 and AC-LIVE/AC-REPLAY on P02: introduce a project-bound Node test variant in `schemas.ts`/runtime configuration, while retaining the existing browser profile, and dispatch it from `StudyService`/`createEnvironment` to a separately owned Node runtime. First target is the real P02 `packages/query-core/src/__tests__/subscribable.test.tsx` at the pinned inspection revision, with a public `/codebook` natural question; do not embed its source or clone in the VSIX. The adapter must launch or attach a VS Code JavaScript debugger session for the repository-local test command, bind source revision/hash and actual DAP session/pause/stack/values, use a visible terminal/test-output capture as the application surface, and feed the existing recording/replay ownership model without fabricated browser frames. It must not demand Chrome or a Next lock for a Node-only test. Preserve immutable evidence, cleanup and stale-session refusal. Expected observable proof: the ordinary chat starts the test through Codebook, pauses in checked-out library code, inspects native Variables/Call Stack, continues to real Vitest outcome, saves a summary/sequence/video, and reopens historical source/debugger/output after runtime exit in both hosts. Falsifiers include a source-only answer, debugger state from another session, missing output video, use of a bundled target or a test process left running after stop. P02 acceptance remains NOT RUN until a complete frozen candidate and Stage 1 campaign exist.

### Repository article capacity blocks public Node entry — selected increment

A normal `/codebook` Copilot Chat **Auto → GPT-6 Luna** trial in the VS Code 1.139.1 development host used the current designated dashboard and `app/lib/data.test.ts`. The agent confirmed `node-test` in `codebook_status`, read its public help/current source, and tried to publish a citation-bound article for the actual 875-cent conversion test. The first publication input had an invalid hash; the agent corrected and retried, but both new-publication and existing-article recovery were blocked: this workspace already retains 16 repository books, and the other articles do not cite the current test. No Codebook-managed Vitest run, debugger stop or new journal occurred. The agent preserved the existing library and `.codebook/`; it reported the limitation accurately. The turn used 1.8 displayed credits and did not report Auto quota exhaustion, so the Claude fallback condition is absent.

Primary AC-LIVE/G01 and AC-REPLAY/G03, dependency AC-SAVE/G04. Source inspection identifies `BOOK_MAX_FILES = 16` in `extension/src/constants.ts` and a hard-coded `16 books` rejection in `RepositoryBookStore.publishOwned`; its `entries()` already guards catalog size. This is distinct from `codebook.savedStudyLimit`, which covers study/recording admission. Freeze the bounded repair: admit a seventeenth distinct book without changing any existing book/revision or weakening source validation, retain a fixed per-workspace safety cap of 64 books, and report that exact cap when reached. Existing revisions must remain writable at the cap, and duplicate v1/v2 files for one book must count once. Update the repository-book capacity regression and public README, compile, run the focused store/protocol checks, then repeat the public Auto path on the same dashboard; the final source/citation and debugger stop must still be independently verified. This does not authorize deleting or overwriting old books and does not make a journal a complete learning replay.

#### Capacity mechanism pass, but Auto cannot discover the new limit

The new 17th/65th capacity regression failed on the old 16-book source, then passed after raising the fixed catalog safety cap to 64; the affected repository-book/protocol run passed **27/27**. A new VS Code development-host instance was loaded and the same Copilot Auto conversation resumed. It confirmed the new instance but saw 16 entries in the repository catalog and stopped without attempting publication, inferring that the old limit still applied. This inference is false for the changed source, but the public `codebook_status.repository.limits` omits any book-count cap, so the agent had no authoritative capacity discovery. The turn used GPT-6 Luna / 0.7 displayed credits; no quota exhaustion, article publication, managed Node test, debugger stop or journal occurred.

Extend this same capacity increment: publish the current fixed `booksPerWorkspace` limit in `codebook_status.repository.limits`, test the public protocol value as a hard-coded 64, then repeat the ordinary Auto attempt without giving the agent a hidden capacity claim. A catalog containing 16 old books must not be confused with a full 64-book library. Keep all existing books unchanged; if publication still fails, preserve the exact error and investigate rather than deleting or reusing unrelated citations.

#### Capacity discovery passed; Node capture and history-resource read failed

The current `codebook_status` now exposes `repository.limits.booksPerWorkspace: 64`; the public protocol regression passes with a hard-coded 64. The exact compiled extension suite passes **1,237/1,237** with zero failures/skips/cancellations (210,736 ms), and `git diff --check` passes. In the designated dashboard's VS Code 1.139.1 Extension Development Host, ordinary Copilot Chat **Auto → GPT-6 Luna** read the public 64 limit and 16-entry catalog, then published one new source-cited article without removing or rewriting the older books. The prior 16-entry admission blocker is resolved in this host. No Copilot credit-exhaustion signal appeared; the Claude fallback was not used.

The first managed Node attempt met an unrelated Study layout guard for a preview tab. The empty Welcome preview and a Codebook-generated historical Captured-source tab were closed through native UI; real source tabs remained. An initial public Restore timed out waiting for the focus document, and a subsequent state-aware Restore reached `layoutConfirmed: true`; the agent returned the reader to live/following before starting once. The Node/Vitest process and native debugger then reached an actual pause at `app/lib/data.ts:225:5` in `fetchInvoiceById`, called from `app/lib/data.test.ts:222:26`. This is a native stop observation only. The public run did not commit a verified `node-test.source-stopped` snapshot or test result: preparation ended with `AbortError`, `finalOutcome: not-demonstrated`, and owned resources were released. The Auto turn reported this boundary and did not retry the test.

The public run/manifest path first returned `STALE_INSTANCE`, then recovered a current status. Following its advertised Node-history resource later returned `ENOENT`. The corresponding new private default-profile history directory exists under the designated dashboard workspace key with header, events and seal; `NodeTestHistoryStore.read()` independently verifies it as **sealed partial, four entries**. A later read-only inspection of the Copilot tool inputs identified the cause of this lookup failure: the 4096-byte page returned a next URI with instance segment `c67c-4b46`, but the agent's 8192-byte request used `c67c-4f46`. The changed single character made the directory name nonexistent. This was a copied-URI error, not journal loss or a proven resource-handler defect. The saved partial journal and pre-existing 17-event history remain untouched. The dashboard tracked tree remains unchanged with only its existing untracked `.codebook/`. No application video, historical native replay, source-grounded explanation or passing Codebook test is claimed.

Read-only timing check: the four sealed entries are output events at 166.416, 166.723, 281.716 and 441.281 ms after the owned process began. No `stop` or `result` entry was committed. Current [OwnedNodeTestRun](../../extension/src/ownedNodeTestRun.ts) source creates one 45,000-ms abort signal before output-surface readiness, native attach, worker binding, breakpoint registration, entry continuation and `captureStop`; the snapshot utility also has a 1,500-ms bounded capture call. The observed `AbortError` is consistent with an aborted wait during this sequence but the available journal alone does not identify its trigger or phase. Do not change either timeout based solely on the visible native pause.

Subsequent read-only recovery of the current Copilot Chat transcript identified the existing public run events without exposing output text: output-surface ready → Node process started → two native sessions started → DAP `pause` → worker verified → DAP `breakpoint` → two `connection closed` debugger errors → both sessions terminated → owned process closed. The run remained `preparing` at 15:55:20.615 UTC and was `stopped` with `AbortError` by 15:55:29.010 UTC. The `codebook_start` request was at 15:54:39.240 UTC, so the failure window contains the shared 45-second deadline at approximately 15:55:24 UTC. This strongly implicates the preparation signal but does not prove which awaited operation used it when it expired. The selected source requested `app/lib/data.ts:224:7`; the native editor showed a pause at `225:5`. `captureStop` requires exact path/line/column equality, which may explain why a visible stop was never accepted. The existing event receipt does not retain the DAP stack frame or the breakpoint ACK position, so the position mismatch is still a hypothesis, not a verified root cause.

The sanitized [Node abort and position evidence](evidence/0029/node-test-abort-position.json) records only event types, bounded times, source coordinates and hashes of the private original journal/transcript. It contains no Vitest output text, chat body, inspected variables or credentials.

Read-only follow-up on the current dashboard `main` at `d2f8b705` confirms that the requested `app/lib/data.ts:224:7` is the `amount: invoice.amount / 100` expression and the visually selected `225:5` is the closing brace of the returned object. `app/lib/data.ts` hashes to `1ddf2dc4bc3fdd74d4cf8c2ce78ca8eca088096e1f56045d2dfd21e980b786cc`; the calling test's `app/lib/data.test.ts` hashes to `f034de61cb175e2be2e8d9f01268bdcd15c23deefb093adb06cf3d676428026b`. The current `OwnedRepositoryBreakpoint` rejects an ACK that explicitly reports a different line or column, but a pending ACK can omit those coordinates; `OwnedNodeTestRun.captureStop` then waits for exact frame path, line and column under the preparation signal. This narrows the plausible failure to an unreported actual frame or a different aborted phase; it does not establish which happened. No run, source edit or journal mutation followed. The mandatory entry remains incomplete because the full active-cycle and state reads were truncated; diagnostic implementation and runtime retry remain gated.

Further static inspection of the same current source confirms the diagnostic blind spot: `captureStop` requests `stackTrace` for every new stop in the bound session, searches its frames for exact path/line/column, and returns `undefined` when all observed frames differ. `waitUntil` then keeps polling under the already-created 45-second preparation signal. The observed native `225:5` pause could therefore be discarded without a checked-position error, but the prior receipt has no raw DAP frame coordinates and cannot establish that this branch caused its `AbortError`. The next controlled observation must distinguish breakpoint ACK, entry-stop continuation, stack-frame mismatch and snapshot capture timing before changing the runtime guard or timeout. This is source-analysis evidence only; no test or runtime outcome changed.
